      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivisions 3 and 16 are repealed by Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 1, section 37, effective for revenue for fiscal year 2026. Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 1, section 37, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.126C.052"><b>126C.052</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.125">126C.125</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.126C.06"><b>126C.06</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.154.0">1999 c 159 s 154</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.69.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 69</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL EDUCATION REVENUE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.10">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.10 GENERAL EDUCATION REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General education revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>The general education revenue for each district equals the sum of the district's basic revenue, extended time revenue, gifted and talented revenue, declining enrollment revenue, local optional revenue, small schools revenue, basic skills revenue, secondary sparsity revenue, elementary sparsity revenue, transportation sparsity revenue, total operating capital revenue, equity revenue, pension adjustment revenue, and transition revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Basic revenue.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The basic revenue for each district equals the formula allowance times the adjusted pupil units for the school year. The formula allowance for fiscal year 2023 is $6,863. The formula allowance for fiscal year 2024 is $7,138. The formula allowance for fiscal year 2025 is $7,281.</p>
          <p>(b) The formula allowance for fiscal year 2026 and later must be rounded to the nearest whole dollar and equals the formula allowance for the previous fiscal year times the greater of:</p>
          <p>(1) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/1.02">1.02</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) one plus the rate of change in inflation calculated in paragraph (c) but not to exceed <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/1.03">1.03</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) In January of the calendar year in which the formula allowance begins, the commissioner of education must calculate the rate of change in inflation equal to the change in the Consumer Price Index for all urban consumers as published by the Bureau of Labor Statistics of the Department of Labor for the average of the fourth calendar quarter of the second prior fiscal year compared to the average of the fourth calendar quarter of the immediately prior fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner must publish the formula allowance by the end of February of each year.</p>
          <p>(e) It is the policy and purpose of the legislature to fund its public schools consistent with its constitutional obligations. To this purpose, the legislature may enact additional increases in the general education basic formula allowance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Extended time revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school district's extended time allowance equals $5,117 for fiscal year 2023 and later.</p>
          <p>(b) A school district's extended time revenue is equal to the product of the extended time allowance in paragraph (a) and the sum of the adjusted pupil units of the district for each pupil in average daily membership in excess of 1.0 and less than 1.2 according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.8">126C.05, subdivision 8</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) Extended time revenue for pupils placed in an on-site education program at the Prairie Lakes Education Center or the Lake Park School, located within the borders of Independent School District No. 347, Willmar, for instruction provided after the end of the preceding regular school year and before the beginning of the following regular school year equals membership hours divided by the minimum annual instructional hours in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05">126C.05</a>, subdivision 15, not to exceed 0.20, times the pupil unit weighting in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05">126C.05</a>, subdivision 1, times the extended time allowance in paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(d) A school district qualifies for extended time revenue for instruction provided after the end of the preceding regular school year and before the beginning of the following regular school year for (1) every pupil attending a day treatment program, and (2) every pupil placed in a children's residential facility, whether the education services are provided on-site or off-site. Extended time revenue under this paragraph equals total membership hours in summer instruction divided by the minimum annual instructional hours in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.15">126C.05, subdivision 15</a>, not to exceed 0.20, times the pupil unit weighting in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.1">126C.05, subdivision 1</a>, times the extended time allowance.</p>
          <p>(e) For purposes of this subdivision, "children's residential facility" means a residential facility for children, including a psychiatric residential treatment facility, licensed by the Department of Human Services or the Department of Corrections and subject to Minnesota Rules, chapter 2960, or an inpatient hospitalization that includes mental health services.</p>
          <p>(f) For purposes of this subdivision, "day treatment program" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a site-based structured mental health program consisting of psychotherapy for three or more individuals and individual or group skills training provided by a team, under the treatment supervision of a mental health professional; or</p>
          <p>(2) any other day treatment program designated by the commissioner of education consistent with the Minnesota Automated Reporting Student System manual, procedure 27.</p>
          <p>(g) A school district's extended time revenue may be used for extended day programs, extended week programs, summer school, vacation break academies such as spring break academies and summer term academies, and other programming authorized under the learning year program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Gifted and talented revenue.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Gifted and talented revenue for each district equals the district's adjusted pupil units for that school year times $13. A school district must reserve gifted and talented revenue and, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.15">120B.15</a>, must spend the revenue only to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) identify gifted and talented students;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) provide education programs for gifted and talented students; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) provide staff development to prepare teachers to best meet the unique needs of gifted and talented students.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.2c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2c.<span class="headnote">Small schools revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school district, not including a charter school, is eligible for small schools revenue equal to the greater of the calculation under paragraph (b) or (d).</p>
          <p>(b) The product of:</p>
          <p>(1) $544;</p>
          <p>(2) the district's adjusted pupil units for that year; and</p>
          <p>(3) the greater of zero or the ratio of (i) 960 less the district's adjusted pupil units for that year, to (ii) 960.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purpose of revenue calculated under paragraph (d), "district" includes a qualifying high school under subdivision 6 that is located in a district with at least two high schools.</p>
          <p>(d) The product of:</p>
          <p>(1) $544;</p>
          <p>(2) the district's adjusted pupil units for that year; and</p>
          <p>(3) the greater of zero or the ratio of (i) 960 less the district's adjusted pupil units for that year, to (ii) 960.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.2d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2d.<span class="headnote">Declining enrollment revenue.</span></h2>
          <p> A school district's declining enrollment revenue equals the greater of zero or the product of: (1) 28 percent of the formula allowance for that year; and (2) the difference between the adjusted pupil units for the preceding year and the adjusted pupil units for the current year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.2e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2e.<span class="headnote">Local optional revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Local optional revenue for a school district equals the sum of the district's first tier local optional revenue and second tier local optional revenue. A district's first tier local optional revenue equals $300 times the adjusted pupil units of the district for that school year. A district's second tier local optional revenue equals $424 times the adjusted pupil units of the district for that school year.</p>
          <p>(b) A district's local optional levy equals the sum of the first tier local optional levy and the second tier local optional levy.</p>
          <p>(c) A district's first tier local optional levy equals the district's first tier local optional revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $880,000.</p>
          <p>(d) For fiscal year 2023, a district's second tier local optional levy equals the district's second tier local optional revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $548,842. For fiscal year 2024, a district's second tier local optional levy equals the district's second tier local optional revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $510,000. For fiscal year 2025, a district's second tier local optional levy equals the district's second tier local optional revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $626,450. For fiscal year 2026, a district's second tier local optional levy equals the district's second tier local optional revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $642,038. For fiscal year 2027 and later, a district's second tier local optional levy equals the district's second tier local optional revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $671,345.</p>
          <p>(e) The local optional levy must be spread on referendum market value. A district may levy less than the permitted amount.</p>
          <p>(f) A district's local optional aid equals its local optional revenue minus its local optional levy. If a district's actual levy for first or second tier local optional revenue is less than its maximum levy limit for that tier, its aid must be proportionately reduced.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Compensatory education revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's compensatory revenue equals the sum of its compensatory revenue for each building in the district and the amounts designated under Laws 2015, First Special Session chapter 3, article 2, section 70, subdivision 8, for fiscal year 2017. Revenue shall be paid to the district and must be allocated according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.15#stat.126C.15.2">126C.15, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For fiscal years 2024 and 2025, the compensatory education revenue for each building in the district equals the formula allowance minus $839 times the compensation revenue pupil units computed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.3">126C.05, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) For fiscal year 2026 and later, the compensatory education revenue for each building in the district equals its compensatory pupils multiplied by the building compensatory allowance.</p>
          <p>(d) When the district contracting with an alternative program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.69">124D.69 </a>changes prior to the start of a school year, the compensatory revenue generated by pupils attending the program shall be paid to the district contracting with the alternative program for the current school year, and shall not be paid to the district contracting with the alternative program for the prior school year.</p>
          <p>(e) When the fiscal agent district for an area learning center changes prior to the start of a school year, the compensatory revenue shall be paid to the fiscal agent district for the current school year, and shall not be paid to the fiscal agent district for the prior school year.</p>
          <p>(f) Notwithstanding paragraph (c), for fiscal year 2026, if the sum of the amounts calculated under paragraph (c) is less than $838,947,000, the commissioner must proportionately increase the revenue to each building until the total statewide revenue calculated for each building equals $838,947,000.</p>
          <p>(g) Notwithstanding paragraph (c), for fiscal year 2027 and later, if the sum of the amounts calculated under paragraph (c) is less than $857,152,000, the commissioner must proportionately increase the revenue to each building until the total statewide revenue calculated for each building equals $857,152,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this subdivision apply only to subdivisions 3, 3b, and 3c.</p>
          <p>(a) "Building compensatory allowance" means a building concentration factor multiplied by the statewide compensatory allowance.</p>
          <p>(b) "Building concentration factor" means the ratio of a building's compensatory pupils to the number of pupils enrolled in the building on October 1 of the previous fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(c) "Compensatory pupils" means the sum of the number of pupils enrolled in a building eligible to receive free meals pursuant to subdivision 3b plus one-half of the pupils eligible to receive reduced-priced meals pursuant to subdivision 3b on October 1 of the previous fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(d) "Statewide compensatory allowance" means the amount calculated pursuant to subdivision 3c.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding paragraphs (b) and (c), for voluntary prekindergarten programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.08">142D.08</a>, charter schools, and contracted alternative programs in the first year of operation, the building concentration factor and compensatory pupils must be computed using data for the current fiscal year. If the voluntary prekindergarten program, charter school, or contracted alternative program begins operation after October 1, the building concentration factor and compensatory pupils must be computed based on pupils enrolled on an alternate date determined by the commissioner and the compensatory pupils must be prorated based on the ratio of the number of days of student instruction to 170 days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Free and reduced-price meals.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning October 1, 2024, the commissioner shall determine the number of children eligible by means of direct certification to receive either a free or reduced-price meal on October 1 each year. Children enrolled in a building on October 1 and determined to be eligible by means of direct certification to receive free or reduced-price meals by December 15 of that school year shall be counted as eligible on October 1 for purposes of subdivision 3. The commissioner must use federal definitions for these purposes. The commissioner may adopt reporting guidelines to assure accuracy of data counts and eligibility. Districts must use any guidelines adopted by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.3c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3c.<span class="headnote">Statewide compensatory allowance.</span></h2>
          <p> For fiscal year 2026, the statewide compensatory allowance is $6,734. For fiscal year 2027 and later, the statewide compensatory allowance equals the statewide compensatory allowance in effect for the prior fiscal year times the ratio of the formula allowance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, for the current fiscal year to the formula allowance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, for the prior fiscal year, rounded to the nearest whole dollar.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Basic skills revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district's basic skills revenue equals the sum of:</p>
          <p>(1) compensatory revenue under subdivision 3; and</p>
          <p>(2) English learner revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.65">124D.65, subdivisions 5 and 5a</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.1.37.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 1 s 37</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this subdivision apply only to subdivisions 7 and 8.</p>
          <p>(a) "High school" means a public secondary school, except a charter school under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124E">124E</a>, that has pupils enrolled in at least the 10th, 11th, and 12th grades. If there is no high school in the district and the school is at least 19 miles from the next nearest school, the commissioner must designate one school in the district as a high school for the purposes of this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Secondary average daily membership" means, for a district that has only one high school, the average daily membership of pupils served in grades 7 through 12. For a district that has more than one high school, "secondary average daily membership" for each high school means the product of the average daily membership of pupils served in grades 7 through 12 in the high school, times the ratio of six to the number of grades in the high school.</p>
          <p>(c) "Attendance area" means the total surface area of the district, in square miles, divided by the number of high schools in the district. For a district that does not operate a high school and is less than 19 miles from the nearest operating high school, the attendance area equals zero.</p>
          <p>(d) "Isolation index" for a high school means the square root of 55 percent of the attendance area plus the distance in miles, according to the usually traveled routes, between the high school and the nearest high school. For a district in which there is located land defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84A.01">84A.01</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84A.20">84A.20</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84A.31">84A.31</a>, the distance in miles is the sum of:</p>
          <p>(1) the square root of one-half of the attendance area; and</p>
          <p>(2) the distance from the border of the district to the nearest high school.</p>
          <p>(e) "Qualifying high school" means a high school that has an isolation index greater than 23 and that has secondary average daily membership of less than 400.</p>
          <p>(f) "Qualifying elementary school" means a public elementary school, except a charter school under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124E">124E</a>, that is located 19 miles or more from the nearest elementary school or from the nearest elementary school within the district and, in either case, has an elementary average daily membership of an average of 20 or fewer per grade.</p>
          <p>(g) "Elementary average daily membership" means, for a district that has only one elementary school, the average daily membership of pupils served in kindergarten through grade 6. For a district that has more than one elementary school, "average daily membership" for each school means the average daily membership of pupils served in kindergarten through grade 6 multiplied by the ratio of seven to the number of grades in the elementary school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Secondary sparsity revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's secondary sparsity revenue for a school year equals the sum of the results of the following calculation for each qualifying high school in the district:</p>
          <p>(1) the formula allowance for the school year minus $530, multiplied by</p>
          <p>(2) the secondary average daily membership of pupils served in the high school, multiplied by</p>
          <p>(3) the quotient obtained by dividing 400 minus the secondary average daily membership by 400 plus the secondary daily membership, multiplied by</p>
          <p>(4) the lesser of 1.5 or the quotient obtained by dividing the isolation index minus 23 by ten.</p>
          <p>(b) A newly formed district that is the result of districts combining under the cooperation and combination program or consolidating under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.48">123A.48</a> must receive secondary sparsity revenue equal to the greater of: (1) the amount calculated under paragraph (a) for the combined district; or (2) the sum of the amounts of secondary sparsity revenue the former districts had in the year prior to consolidation, increased for any subsequent changes in the secondary sparsity formula.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Elementary sparsity revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's elementary sparsity revenue equals the sum of the following amounts for each qualifying elementary school in the district:</p>
          <p>(1) the formula allowance for the year minus $530, multiplied by</p>
          <p>(2) the elementary average daily membership of pupils served in the school, multiplied by</p>
          <p>(3) the quotient obtained by dividing 140 minus the elementary average daily membership by 140 plus the average daily membership.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Sparsity revenue for school districts that close facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district that closes a school facility or whose sparsity revenue is reduced by a school closure in another district is eligible for elementary and secondary sparsity revenue equal to the greater of the amounts calculated under subdivisions 6, 7, and 8 or the total amount of sparsity revenue for the previous fiscal year if the school board of the district has adopted a written resolution stating that the district intends to close the school facility, but cannot proceed with the closure without the adjustment to sparsity revenue authorized by this subdivision. The written resolution must be approved by the board and filed with the commissioner of education prior to the start of the fiscal year for which aid under this subdivision is first requested. A school district whose sparsity revenue is affected by a closure in another district is not required to adopt a written resolution under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a); <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.55.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 55</a> subd 1]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Total operating capital revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Total operating capital revenue for a district equals the sum of:</p>
          <p>(1) $79 times the adjusted pupil units for the school year;</p>
          <p>(2) the product of $109, the district's maintenance cost index, and its adjusted pupil units for the school year plus the amount computed under paragraph (c); and</p>
          <p>(3) $2 times the adjusted pupil units of the school district for the school year for the purposes of supplying menstrual products under subdivision 14, clause (26), and opiate antagonists under subdivision 14, clause (27).</p>
          <p>(b) The revenue under this subdivision must be placed in a reserved account in the general fund and may only be used according to subdivision 14.</p>
          <p>(c) The revenue under paragraph (a), clause (2), for a district that operates a program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.128">124D.128</a>, is increased by an amount equal to $31 times the number of adjusted pupil units served at the site where the program is implemented.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.13a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13a.<span class="headnote">Operating capital levy.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To obtain operating capital revenue, a district may levy an amount not more than the product of its operating capital equalization revenue for the fiscal year times the lesser of one or the ratio of its adjusted net tax capacity per adjusted pupil unit to the operating capital equalizing factor. The operating capital equalizing factor equals $22,912 for fiscal year 2024, $23,138 for fiscal year 2025, and $22,912 for fiscal year 2026 and later.</p>
          <p>(b) A district's operating capital equalization revenue equals the district's total operating capital revenue under subdivision 13, calculated without the amount under subdivision 13, paragraph (a), clause (3).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.13b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13b.<span class="headnote">Operating capital aid.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's operating capital aid equals its operating capital revenue minus its operating capital levy times the ratio of the actual amount levied to the permitted levy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Uses of total operating capital revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Total operating capital revenue may be used only for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) to acquire land for school purposes;</p>
          <p>(2) to acquire or construct buildings for school purposes;</p>
          <p>(3) to rent or lease buildings, including the costs of building repair or improvement that are part of a lease agreement;</p>
          <p>(4) to improve and repair school sites and buildings, and equip or reequip school buildings with permanent attached fixtures, including library media centers and gender-neutral single-user restrooms, locker room privacy stalls, or other spaces with privacy features, including single-user shower stalls, changing stalls, or other single-user facilities;</p>
          <p>(5) for a surplus school building that is used substantially for a public nonschool purpose;</p>
          <p>(6) to eliminate barriers or increase access to school buildings by individuals with a disability;</p>
          <p>(7) to bring school buildings into compliance with the State Fire Code adopted according to chapter 299F;</p>
          <p>(8) to remove asbestos from school buildings, encapsulate asbestos, or make asbestos-related repairs;</p>
          <p>(9) to clean up and dispose of polychlorinated biphenyls found in school buildings;</p>
          <p>(10) to clean up, remove, dispose of, and make repairs related to storing heating fuel or transportation fuels such as alcohol, gasoline, fuel oil, and special fuel, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/296A.01">296A.01</a>;</p>
          <p>(11) for energy audits for school buildings and to modify buildings if the audit indicates the cost of the modification can be recovered within ten years;</p>
          <p>(12) to improve buildings that are leased according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.51#stat.123B.51.4">123B.51, subdivision 4</a>;</p>
          <p>(13) to pay special assessments levied against school property but not to pay assessments for service charges;</p>
          <p>(14) to pay principal and interest on state loans for energy conservation according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.37">216C.37</a> or loans made under the Douglas J. Johnson Economic Protection Trust Fund Act according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.292">298.292</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.297">298.297</a>;</p>
          <p>(15) to purchase or lease interactive telecommunications equipment;</p>
          <p>(16) by board resolution, to transfer money into the debt redemption fund to: (i) pay the amounts needed to meet, when due, principal and interest payments on certain obligations issued according to chapter 475; or (ii) pay principal and interest on debt service loans or capital loans according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.70">126C.70</a>;</p>
          <p>(17) to pay operating capital-related assessments of any entity formed under a cooperative agreement between two or more districts;</p>
          <p>(18) to purchase or lease computers and related hardware, software, and annual licensing fees, copying machines, telecommunications equipment, and other noninstructional equipment;</p>
          <p>(19) to purchase or lease assistive technology or equipment for instructional programs;</p>
          <p>(20) to purchase textbooks as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.41#stat.123B.41.2">123B.41, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>(21) to purchase new and replacement library media resources or technology;</p>
          <p>(22) to lease or purchase vehicles;</p>
          <p>(23) to purchase or lease telecommunications equipment, computers, and related equipment for integrated information management systems for:</p>
          <p>(i) managing and reporting learner outcome information for all students under a results-oriented graduation rule;</p>
          <p>(ii) managing student assessment, services, and achievement information required for students with individualized education programs; and</p>
          <p>(iii) other classroom information management needs;</p>
          <p>(24) to pay personnel costs directly related to the acquisition, operation, and maintenance of telecommunications systems, computers, related equipment, and network and applications software;</p>
          <p>(25) to pay the costs directly associated with closing a school facility, including moving and storage costs;</p>
          <p>(26) to pay the costs of supplies and equipment necessary to provide access to menstrual products at no charge to students in restrooms and as otherwise needed in school facilities; and</p>
          <p>(27) to pay the costs of the opiate antagonists required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.224">121A.224</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Uses of revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise prohibited by law, a district may spend general fund money for capital purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Maintenance cost index.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's maintenance cost index is equal to the ratio of:</p>
          <p>(1) the total weighted square footage for all eligible district-owned facilities; and</p>
          <p>(2) the total unweighted square footage of these facilities.</p>
          <p>(b) The department shall determine a district's maintenance cost index annually. Eligible district-owned facilities must include only instructional or administrative square footage owned by the district. The commissioner may adjust the age of a building or addition for major renovation projects.</p>
          <p>(c) The square footage weighting factor for each original building or addition equals the lesser of:</p>
          <p>(1) one plus the ratio of the age in years to 100; or</p>
          <p>(2) 1.5.</p>
          <p>(d) The weighted square footage for each original building or addition equals the product of the unweighted square footage times the square footage weighting factor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Transportation sparsity definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this subdivision apply to subdivision 18.</p>
          <p>(a) "Sparsity index" for a district means the greater of .2 or the ratio of the square mile area of the district to the resident pupil units of the district.</p>
          <p>(b) "Density index" for a district means the ratio of the square mile area of the district to the resident pupil units of the district. However, the density index for a district cannot be greater than .2 or less than .005.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Transportation sparsity revenue allowance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's transportation sparsity allowance equals the greater of zero or the result of the following computation:</p>
          <p>(i) Multiply the formula allowance according to subdivision 2, by .141.</p>
          <p>(ii) Multiply the result in clause (i) by the district's sparsity index raised to the 26/100 power.</p>
          <p>(iii) Multiply the result in clause (ii) by the district's density index raised to the 13/100 power.</p>
          <p>(iv) Multiply the formula allowance according to subdivision 2, by .0466.</p>
          <p>(v) Subtract the result in clause (iv) from the result in clause (iii).</p>
          <p>(b) Transportation sparsity revenue is equal to the transportation sparsity allowance times the adjusted pupil units.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.18a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18a.<span class="headnote">Pupil transportation adjustment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An independent, common, or special school district's transportation sparsity revenue under subdivision 18 is increased by the greater of zero or 35 percent of the difference between:</p>
          <p>(1) the lesser of the district's total cost for regular and excess pupil transportation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.92#stat.123B.92.1">123B.92, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), including depreciation, for the previous fiscal year or 105 percent of the district's total cost for the second previous fiscal year; and</p>
          <p>(2) the sum of:</p>
          <p>(i) 4.66 percent of the district's basic revenue for the previous fiscal year;</p>
          <p>(ii) transportation sparsity revenue under subdivision 18 for the previous fiscal year;</p>
          <p>(iii) the district's charter school transportation adjustment for the previous fiscal year;</p>
          <p>(iv) the district's reimbursement for transportation provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.92">123B.92</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b), clause (1), item (vi); and</p>
          <p>(v) the district's area learning center transportation aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.92">123B.92, subdivision </a>11.</p>
          <p>(b) A charter school's pupil transportation adjustment equals the school district per pupil unit adjustment under paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.55.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 55</a> subd 1]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">Equity revenue.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A school district qualifies for equity revenue if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the school district's adjusted pupil unit amount of basic revenue, transition revenue, first tier local optional revenue, and referendum revenue is less than the value of the school district at or immediately above the 95th percentile of school districts in its equity region for those revenue categories; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the school district's administrative offices are not located in a city of the first class on July 1, 1999.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Equity revenue equals the product of (1) the district's adjusted pupil units for that year; times (2) the sum of (i) $14, plus (ii) $80, times the school district's equity index computed under subdivision 27.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A school district's equity revenue is increased by the greater of zero or an amount equal to the district's adjusted pupil units times the difference between ten percent of the statewide average amount of referendum revenue and first tier local optional revenue per adjusted pupil unit for that year and the sum of the district's referendum revenue and first tier local optional revenue per adjusted pupil unit. A school district's revenue under this paragraph must not exceed $100,000 for that year.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A school district's equity revenue for a school district located in the metro equity region equals the amount computed in paragraphs (b) and (c) multiplied by 1.25.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) For fiscal year 2020 and later for a school district not included in paragraph (d), a district's equity revenue equals the amount computed in paragraphs (b) and (c) multiplied by 1.25.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) A school district's additional equity revenue equals $50 times its adjusted pupil units.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.<span class="headnote">Regional equity gap.</span></h2>
          <p>The regional equity gap equals the difference between the value of the school district at or immediately above the fifth percentile of adjusted general revenue per adjusted pupil unit and the value of the school district at or immediately above the 95th percentile of adjusted general revenue per adjusted pupil unit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">District equity gap.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's equity gap equals the greater of zero or the difference between the district's adjusted general revenue and the value of the school district at or immediately above the regional 95th percentile of adjusted general revenue per adjusted pupil unit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">District equity index.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's equity index equals the ratio of the sum of the district equity gap amount to the regional equity gap amount.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Equity region.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of computing equity revenue under subdivision 24, a district with its administrative office located in Anoka, Carver, Dakota, Hennepin, Ramsey, Scott, or Washington County on January 1, 2012, is part of the metro equity region. All other districts are part of the rural equity region.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.29">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 29.<span class="headnote">Equity levy.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain equity revenue for fiscal year 2015 and later, a district may levy an amount not more than the product of its equity revenue for the fiscal year times the lesser of one or the ratio of its referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $510,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.30">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30.<span class="headnote">Equity aid.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's equity aid equals its equity revenue minus its equity levy times the ratio of the actual amount levied to the permitted levy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.31">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31.<span class="headnote">Transition revenue.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A district's transition allowance equals the sum of the transition revenue the district would have received for fiscal year 2015 under Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10">126C.10, subdivisions 31</a>, 31a, and 31c, and the greater of zero or the difference between:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the sum of:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) the general education revenue the district would have received for fiscal year 2015 according to Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10">126C.10</a>;</p>
          <p>(ii) the integration revenue the district received for fiscal year 2013 under Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.86">124D.86</a>;</p>
          <p>(iii) the pension adjustment the district would have received for fiscal year 2015 under Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.50">127A.50</a>;</p>
          <p>(iv) the special education aid the district would have received for fiscal year 2015 under Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a>; and</p>
          <p>(v) the special education excess cost aid the district would have received for fiscal year 2015 under Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.79">125A.79</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) the sum of the district's:</p>
          <p>(i) general education revenue for fiscal year 2015 excluding transition revenue under this section;</p>
          <p>(ii) achievement and integration revenue for fiscal year 2015 under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a>;</p>
          <p>(iii) special education aid for fiscal year 2015 under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a>; and</p>
          <p>(iv) alternative teacher compensation revenue for fiscal year 2015 under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.415">122A.415</a>,</p>
          <p class="bl">divided by the number of adjusted pupil units for fiscal year 2015.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A district's transition revenue for fiscal year 2015 and later equals the product of the district's transition allowance times the district's adjusted pupil units.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.31a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.59.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.31b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31b.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.59.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.31c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31c.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.59.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.32">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32.<span class="headnote">Transition levy.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain transition revenue for fiscal year 2015 and later, a district may levy an amount not more than the product of its transition revenue for the fiscal year times the lesser of one or the ratio of its referendum market value per resident pupil unit to $510,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.33">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33.<span class="headnote">Transition aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For fiscal year 2004, a district's transition aid equals its transition revenue.</p>
          <p>(b) For fiscal year 2005 and later, a district's transition aid equals its transition revenue minus its transition levy times the ratio of the actual amount levied to the permitted levy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.34">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 34.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.59.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.35">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 35.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.59.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.36">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 36.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.59.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.10.37">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37.<span class="headnote">Pension adjustment revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school district's pension adjustment revenue equals the sum of:</p>
          <p>(1) the greater of zero or the product of:</p>
          <p>(i) the difference between the district's adjustment under Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.50#stat.127A.50.1">127A.50, subdivision 1</a>, for fiscal year 2014 per adjusted pupil unit and the state average adjustment under Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.50#stat.127A.50.1">127A.50, subdivision 1</a>, for fiscal year 2014 per adjusted pupil unit; and</p>
          <p>(ii) the district's adjusted pupil units for the fiscal year; and</p>
          <p>(2) the product of the salaries paid to district employees who were members of the Teachers Retirement Association and the St. Paul Teachers' Retirement Fund Association for the prior fiscal year and the district's pension adjustment rate for the fiscal year. The pension adjustment rate for Independent School District No. 625, St. Paul, equals 2.3 percent for fiscal year 2023, 2.5 percent for fiscal year 2024 and fiscal year 2025, and 3.25 percent for fiscal year 2026 and later. The pension adjustment rate for all other districts equals 1.05 percent for fiscal year 2023, 1.25 percent for fiscal year 2024 and fiscal year 2025, and 2.0 percent for fiscal year 2026 and later.</p>
          <p>(b) For fiscal year 2025, the state total pension adjustment revenue under paragraph (a), clause (2), must not exceed the amount calculated under paragraph (a), clause (2), for fiscal year 2024. The commissioner must prorate the pension adjustment revenue under paragraph (a), clause (2), so as not to exceed the maximum.</p>
          <p>(c) For fiscal year 2026 and fiscal year 2027, the state total pension adjustment revenue under paragraph (a), clause (2), must not be prorated.</p>
          <p>(d) For fiscal year 2028 and later, the state total pension adjustment revenue under paragraph (a), clause (2), must not exceed the amount calculated under paragraph (a), clause (2), for fiscal year 2027. The commissioner must prorate the pension adjustment revenue under paragraph (a), clause (2), so as not to exceed the maximum.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.26#stat.123A.26.1">123A.26, subdivision 1</a>, a cooperative unit, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, qualifies for pension adjustment revenue under paragraph (a), clause (2), as if it was a district, and the aid generated by the cooperative unit shall be paid to the cooperative unit.</p>
        </div>
      </div>

<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.1.10.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 1 s 10</a>-14; art 8 s 8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/116/#laws.1.2.0">2020 c 116 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/13/#laws.1.4.0">1Sp2021 c 13 art 1 s 4</a>,5; art 9 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.1.10.0">2023 c 55 art 1 s 10</a>-20; art 8 s 12; art 10 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.14.1.0">2023 c 64 art 14 s 1</a>;<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.1.7.0">2024 c 115 art 1 s 7</a>-13; art 16 s 42</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.126C.11"><b>126C.11</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (a)]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.12">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.12 LEARNING AND DEVELOPMENT REVENUE AMOUNT AND USE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.12.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Of a district's general education revenue for fiscal year 2015 and thereafter each school district shall reserve an amount equal to the following calculation:</p>
          <p>(1) average daily membership, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.5">126C.05, subdivision 5</a>, in kindergarten times $299; plus</p>
          <p>(2) average daily membership, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.5">126C.05, subdivision 5</a>, in grades 1 to 6 times $459.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.12.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Classroom teacher" means a public employee licensed by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board who is authorized to teach all subjects to children in any grade in kindergarten through grade 6 and whose duties are full-time regular classroom instruction, excluding a teacher for whom federal aids are received or for whom categorical aids are received under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.76">125A.76</a> or who is an itinerant teacher or provides instruction outside of the regular classroom. Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.68#stat.122A.68.6">122A.68, subdivision 6</a>, classroom teacher does not include supervisory and support personnel defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.15">122A.15</a>. A classroom teacher whose duties are less than full-time instruction must be included as an equivalent only for the number of hours of instruction in kindergarten through grade 3.</p>
          <p>(b) "Class size" means the districtwide ratio at each grade level of the number of full-time students in kindergarten through grade 3 served at least 40 percent of the time in regular classrooms to the number of full-time classroom teachers in kindergarten through grade 3, determined as of October 1 of each school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.12.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Instruction contact time.</span></h2>
          <p>Instruction may be provided by a classroom teacher or by a team of classroom teachers, or by a teacher resident supervised by a classroom teacher. The district must maximize classroom teacher to learner average instructional contact time in the core subjects of reading and mathematics.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.12.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Revenue use.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Revenue must be used to reduce and maintain the district's average class size in kindergarten through grade 3 to a level of 17 to 1 on average in each of the respective grades.</p>
          <p>(b) A district must not increase the districtwide class sizes in other grades as a result of reducing class sizes in kindergarten through grade 3. Revenue may not be used to provide instructor preparation. A district may use a portion of the revenue reserved under this section to employ up to the same number of full-time equivalent education assistants or aides as the district employed during the 1992-1993 school year under Minnesota Statutes 1992, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124.331#stat.124.331.2">124.331, subdivision 2</a>, through fiscal year 2002. Beginning in fiscal year 2003, class size reduction revenue may only be reserved to employ classroom teachers contributing to lower class sizes in kindergarten through grade 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.12.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Additional revenue use.</span></h2>
          <p>If the board of a district determines that the district has achieved and is maintaining the class sizes specified in subdivision 4, the board may use the revenue to reduce class size in grades 4, 5, and 6, prepare and use individualized learning plans, improve program offerings, purchase instructional material, services, or technology, or provide staff development needed for reduced class sizes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.12.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.1.28.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 1 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.12.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district must annually report the district's class size ratios by each grade to the commissioner of education in the form and manner specified by the commissioner. The department must annually submit a report beginning December 1, 2024, to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over kindergarten through grade 12 education detailing the statewide ratios by grade starting with the 2023-2024 school year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.1.18.0">1993 c 224 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/374/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 374 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.1.25.0">1994 c 647 art 1 s 25</a>; art 7 s 2; art 8 s 12,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.1.45.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 1 s 45</a>; art 13 s 8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.66.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.11.3.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.152.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 152</a>,153,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.1.35.0">1999 c 241 art 1 s 35</a>,36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/464/#laws.3.5.0">2000 c 464 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.2.21.0">2000 c 489 art 2 s 21</a>; art 10 s 14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.22.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 22</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.40.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 40</a>,41; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.22.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.1.21.0">2023 c 55 art 1 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.126C.125"><b>126C.125</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.54.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 54</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.126">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.126 USE OF GENERAL EDUCATION REVENUE FOR ALL-DAY KINDERGARTEN AND PREKINDERGARTEN.</h1>
        <p class="in">A school district may spend general education revenue on extended time kindergarten and prekindergarten programs. At the school board's discretion, the district may use revenue generated by the all-day kindergarten pupil count under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05">126C.05</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (c), to meet the needs of three- and four-year-olds in the district. A school district may not use these funds on programs for three- and four-year-old children while maintaining a fee-based all-day kindergarten program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.126.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.27.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.1.10.0">2007 c 146 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.1.20.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.1.25.0">2012 c 239 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.42.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.29.0">2024 c 85 s 29</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.13">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.13 GENERAL EDUCATION AID.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (b)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (b)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 30</a> para (b)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2016 [Repealed, 1Sp2015 c 3 art 1 s 28]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.</h2>
          <p>MS 2016 [Repealed, 1Sp2015 c 3 art 1 s 28]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.3c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3c.</h2>
          <p>MS 2016 [Repealed, 1Sp2015 c 3 art 1 s 28]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">General education aid.</span></h2>
          <p>For fiscal year 2015 and later, a district's general education aid equals:</p>
          <p>(1) general education revenue, excluding operating capital revenue, equity revenue, local optional revenue, and transition revenue; plus</p>
          <p>(2) operating capital aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.13b">126C.10, subdivision 13b</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) equity aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.30">126C.10, subdivision 30</a>; plus</p>
          <p>(4) transition aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.33">126C.10, subdivision 33</a>; plus</p>
          <p>(5) shared time aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.01#stat.126C.01.7">126C.01, subdivision 7</a>; plus</p>
          <p>(6) referendum aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.17">126C.17, subdivisions 7</a> and 7a; plus</p>
          <p>(7) online learning aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.096">124D.096</a>; plus</p>
          <p>(8) local optional aid according to section 126C.10, subdivision 2e, paragraph (f).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.13.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Uses of revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.14">126C.10, subdivision 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.12">126C.12</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.15">126C.15</a>, general education revenue may be used during the regular school year and the summer for general and special school purposes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.13.history
			">

      <div class="sr" id="stat.126C.14"><b>126C.14</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.54.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 54</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.15">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.15 BASIC SKILLS REVENUE; COMPENSATORY EDUCATION REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.15.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Use of revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The basic skills revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.4">126C.10, subdivision 4</a>, must be reserved and used to meet the educational needs of pupils who enroll under-prepared to learn and whose progress toward meeting state or local content or performance standards is below the level that is appropriate for learners of their age. Basic skills revenue may also be used for programs designed to prepare children and their families for entry into school whether the student first enrolls in kindergarten or first grade.</p>
          <p>(b) For fiscal years prior to fiscal year 2024, any of the following may be provided to meet these learners' needs:</p>
          <p>(1) direct instructional services under the assurance of mastery program according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.66">124D.66</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) remedial instruction in reading, language arts, mathematics, other content areas, or study skills to improve the achievement level of these learners;</p>
          <p>(3) additional teachers and teacher aides to provide more individualized instruction to these learners through individual tutoring, lower instructor-to-learner ratios, or team teaching;</p>
          <p>(4) a longer school day or week during the regular school year or through a summer program that may be offered directly by the site or under a performance-based contract with a community-based organization;</p>
          <p>(5) comprehensive and ongoing staff development consistent with district and site plans according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60">122A.60</a> and to implement plans under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.12">120B.12</a>, subdivision 4a, for teachers, teacher aides, principals, and other personnel to improve their ability to identify the needs of these learners and provide appropriate remediation, intervention, accommodations, or modifications;</p>
          <p>(6) instructional materials, digital learning, and technology appropriate for meeting the individual needs of these learners;</p>
          <p>(7) programs to reduce truancy, encourage completion of high school, enhance self-concept, provide health services, provide nutrition services, provide a safe and secure learning environment, provide coordination for pupils receiving services from other governmental agencies, provide psychological services to determine the level of social, emotional, cognitive, and intellectual development, and provide counseling services, guidance services, and social work services;</p>
          <p>(8) bilingual programs, bicultural programs, and programs for English learners;</p>
          <p>(9) all-day kindergarten;</p>
          <p>(10) early education programs, parent-training programs, school readiness programs, kindergarten programs for four-year-olds, voluntary home visits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.10#stat.142D.10.4">142D.10, subdivision 4</a>, and other outreach efforts designed to prepare children for kindergarten;</p>
          <p>(11) extended school day and extended school year programs; and</p>
          <p>(12) substantial parent involvement in developing and implementing remedial education or intervention plans for a learner, including learning contracts between the school, the learner, and the parent that establish achievement goals and responsibilities of the learner and the learner's parent or guardian.</p>
          <p>(c) For fiscal year 2024 and later, a district's basic skills revenue must be used for:</p>
          <p>(1) remedial instruction and necessary materials in reading, language arts, mathematics, other content areas, or study skills to improve the achievement level of these learners;</p>
          <p>(2) additional teachers and teacher aides to provide more individualized instruction to these learners through individual tutoring, lower instructor-to-learner ratios, or team teaching;</p>
          <p>(3) a longer school day or week during the regular school year or through a summer program that may be offered directly by the site or under a performance-based contract with a community-based organization;</p>
          <p>(4) programs to reduce truancy; provide counseling services, guidance services, and social work services; and provide coordination for pupils receiving services from other governmental agencies;</p>
          <p>(5) bilingual programs, bicultural programs, and programs for English learners;</p>
          <p>(6) early education programs, parent-training programs, early childhood special education, school readiness programs, kindergarten programs for four-year-olds, voluntary home visits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.10#stat.142D.10.4">142D.10, subdivision 4</a>, and other outreach efforts designed to prepare children for kindergarten;</p>
          <p>(7) transition programs operated by school districts for special education students until the age of 22;</p>
          <p>(8) substantial parent involvement in developing and implementing remedial education or intervention plans for a learner, including learning contracts between the school, the learner, and the parent that establish achievement goals and responsibilities of the learner and the learner's parent or guardian; and</p>
          <p>(9) professional development for teachers on meeting the needs of English learners, using assessment tools and data to monitor student progress, and reducing the use of exclusionary discipline, and training for tutors and staff in extended day programs to enhance staff's knowledge in content areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.15.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Building allocation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A district or cooperative must allocate at least 80 percent of its compensatory revenue to each school building in the district or cooperative where the children who have generated the revenue are served unless the school district or cooperative has received permission under Laws 2005, First Special Session chapter 5, article 1, section 50, to allocate compensatory revenue according to student performance measures developed by the school board.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A district or cooperative may allocate no more than 20 percent of the amount of compensatory revenue that the district receives to school sites according to a plan adopted by the school board. The money reallocated under this paragraph must be spent for the purposes listed in subdivision 1, but may be spent on students in any grade, including students attending school readiness or other prekindergarten programs.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For the purposes of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.3">126C.05, subdivision 3</a>, "building" means education site as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.04#stat.123B.04.1">123B.04, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Notwithstanding section 123A.26, subdivision 1, compensatory revenue generated by students served at a cooperative unit shall be paid to the cooperative unit.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) A district or cooperative with school building openings, school building closings, changes in attendance area boundaries, or other changes in programs or student demographics between the prior year and the current year may reallocate compensatory revenue among sites to reflect these changes. A district or cooperative must report to the department any adjustments it makes according to this paragraph and the department must use the adjusted compensatory revenue allocations in preparing the report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.76#stat.123B.76.3">123B.76, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.15.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Recommendation.</span></h2>
          <p>A school site decision-making team, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.04#stat.123B.04.2">123B.04, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), or the instruction and curriculum advisory committee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>, if the school has no school site decision team, shall recommend how the compensatory education revenue will be used to carry out the purpose of this section. A school district that has received permission under Laws 2005, First Special Session chapter 5, article 1, section 50, to allocate compensatory revenue according to school performance measures shall share its plan for the distribution of compensatory revenue with the school site decision team.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.15.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Separate accounts.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district and cooperative unit that receives basic skills revenue shall maintain separate accounts to identify expenditures for salaries and programs related to basic skills revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.15.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Annual expenditure report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By February 1 annually, the commissioner of education must report to the legislature the expenditures of each district that received basic skills revenue in the previous fiscal year under subdivision 1. The report must conform to uniform financial and reporting standards established for this purpose and provide a breakdown by functional area.</p>
          <p>(b) A district must also report whether programs funded with compensatory revenue are consistent with best practices demonstrated to improve student achievement.</p>
          <p>(c) The Department of Education and regional centers of excellence must identify and provide to schools best practices for implementing programs for each use of revenue specified in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.15.history
			">
