<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.7.11.0">1987 c 268 art 7 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.5.84.0">1988 c 719 art 5 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.13.2.0">1989 c 329 art 13 s 2</a>,<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.0.20.0">20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.11.2.0">1990 c 562 art 11 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.5.13.0">1993 c 224 art 5 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.1.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.45.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 45</a>-47,<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.164.0">164</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.4.14.0">1999 c 241 art 4 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.20.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.55.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 55</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1Sp/6/#laws.4.16.0">art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.4.18.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 4 s 18</a>,<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1Sp/9/#laws.4.19.0">19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.4.16.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 4 s 16</a>,<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1Sp/5/#laws.4.17.0">17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/292/#laws.1.11.0">2012 c 292 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.14.110.0">2013 c 143 art 14 s 110</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.30.21.0">2016 c 189 art 30 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.7.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.64">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.64 NET DEBT; DETERMINATION.</h1>
        <p>In computing "net debt" and in determining whether a district is eligible for a state loan, state loans to the district shall not be considered, notwithstanding the provisions of any other general or special law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/583/#laws.0.7.0">1967 c 583 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.48.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 48</a>,164</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.65">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.65 FUND ESTABLISHED; DIVISION INTO ACCOUNTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.65.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Maximum effort school loan fund.</span></h2>
          <p>A "maximum effort school loan fund" for administration of moneys to be received and disbursed as authorized and required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.61">126C.61</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.72">126C.72</a> must be maintained in the state treasury. The fund must be divided into three accounts for the purposes specified in subdivisions 2, 3, 4, and 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.65.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.40.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.65.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Capital loan account.</span></h2>
          <p>A capital loan account must be maintained out of which loans under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.69">126C.69</a> must be made. On November 1 all moneys in the debt service loan account in excess of those for debt service loans then agreed to be made must be transferred to the capital loan account. On July 1, all moneys in the capital loan account in excess of those required for capital loans theretofore agreed to be made must be transferred to the debt service loan account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.65.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Loan repayment account.</span></h2>
          <p>A loan repayment account must be maintained. All principal and interest paid by districts on debt service loans and capital loans made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.69">126C.69</a> must be paid into the account. The state's cost of administering the Maximum Effort School Aid Law must be paid out of this account, to an amount not exceeding $10,000 in any year. As soon as possible in each year after the committee has determined the ratio existing between the correct market value of all taxable property in each school district in the state and the "market value in money" of such property as recorded in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.91">270C.91</a>, the commissioner of revenue shall prepare a list of all such ratios. The clerical costs of preparing the list must be paid as a cost of administration of the Maximum Effort School Aid Law. The Documents Division of the Department of Administration may publish and sell copies of the list. The sums required to pay the principal of and interest on all school loan bonds as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.72">126C.72</a> must be transferred out of the loan repayment account to the state bond fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.65.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Excess money in loan repayment account.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall transfer from the loan repayment account to the credit of the debt service loan account on November 1 of each year all money deposited to the credit of the loan repayment account that is not required for the payment of principal and interest and costs as prescribed in subdivision 4 but that is needed for debt service loans in the fiscal year beginning July 1, and those moneys are annually appropriated to that account for the purposes prescribed by the Maximum Effort School Aid Law. Money deposited to the credit of the loan repayment account and not required for the transfers or for the payment of principal and interest due on school loan bonds may be invested and reinvested in securities which are general obligations of the United States or the state of Minnesota. When all school loan bonds have been fully paid with interest accrued thereon, the balance remaining in the account must be transferred to the state bond fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.65.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/27/#laws.0.4.0">Ex1959 c 27 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/752/#laws.0.1.0">1961 c 752 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/601/#laws.0.2.0">1963 c 601 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/875/#laws.0.3.0">1965 c 875 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/339/#laws.0.8.0">1975 c 339 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.9.2.0">1981 c 358 art 9 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.11.3.0">1990 c 562 art 11 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/45/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 45 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.1.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.49.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 49</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.33.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.66">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.66 APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.66.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to all sums which have been or may hereafter be appropriated by any law, the net proceeds of sale of any state school loan bonds authorized to be issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.72">126C.72</a>, and all income received from the investment of said net proceeds is hereby appropriated to the school loan bond account in the state bond fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.66.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Remaining money.</span></h2>
          <p>Any amounts remaining in the fund on July 1, including any unused portion of the appropriation made in subdivision 1, must be available for use by the commissioner in making further debt service loans and capital loans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.66.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Payments.</span></h2>
          <p>All payments on capital loan contracts, as received by the commissioner, are appropriated to the loan repayment account.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/27/#laws.0.5.0">Ex1959 c 27 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/601/#laws.0.3.0">1963 c 601 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/583/#laws.0.2.0">1967 c 583 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.9.3.0">1981 c 358 art 9 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/45/#laws.0.3.0">1991 c 45 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.50.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 50</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.8.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.67">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.67 SCHOOL LOANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.67.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Consideration by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall receive and consider applications for and grant or deny loans under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.61">126C.61</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.72">126C.72</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.67.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application forms; rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, with the assistance of the attorney general or a designated assistant, shall prepare forms of applications for debt service loans and capital loans and instruments evidencing the loans. The commissioner must promulgate rules to facilitate the commissioner's operations in compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.61">126C.61</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.72">126C.72</a>. The rules are subject to chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.67.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Clerk.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may employ a clerk to administer the Maximum Effort School Aid Law. The commissioner may fix the clerk's compensation, which must be paid out of the loan repayment account of the fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.67.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/27/#laws.0.6.0">Ex1959 c 27 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/562/#laws.0.6.0">1961 c 562 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/6/#laws.0.22.0">1969 c 6 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/61/#laws.0.9.0">1975 c 61 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.30.0">1975 c 162 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.60.0">1976 c 2 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/706/#laws.0.34.0">1978 c 706 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.9.4.0">1981 c 358 art 9 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/560/#laws.0.44.0">1982 c 560 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.1.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.56.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.5.16.0">1997 c 187 art 5 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.51.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 51</a>,52,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.126C.68">
        <h1>126C.68</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.68.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.40.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.68.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.40.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.68.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2019 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.40.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.68.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.40.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.69">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.69 CAPITAL GRANTS AND LOANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Capital grant and loan requests and uses.</span></h2>
          <p>Capital grants and loans are available only to qualifying districts. Capital grants and loans must not be used for the construction of swimming pools, ice arenas, athletic facilities, auditoriums, bus garages, or heating system improvements. Proceeds of the grants and loans may be used only for sites for education facilities and for acquiring, bettering, furnishing, or equipping education facilities. Contracts must be entered into within 18 months after the date on which each grant and loan is approved. For purposes of this section, "education facilities" includes space for Head Start programs and social service programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Capital grant and loan eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning July 1, 2020, a district is not eligible for a capital grant and loan unless the district's estimated net debt tax rate as computed by the commissioner after debt service equalization aid would be more than 41.98 percent of adjusted net tax capacity. The estimate must assume a 20-year maturity schedule for new debt.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">District request for review and comment.</span></h2>
          <p>A district or a joint powers district that intends to apply for a capital grant and loan must submit a proposal to the commissioner for review and comment according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.71">123B.71</a> by July 1 of an odd-numbered year. The commissioner shall prepare a review and comment on the proposed facility, regardless of the amount of the capital expenditure required to construct the facility. In addition to the information provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.71#stat.123B.71.9">123B.71, subdivision 9</a>, the commissioner shall require that predesign packages comparable to those required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.335">16B.335</a> be prepared by the applicant school district. The predesign packages must be sufficient to define the scope, cost, and schedule of the project and must demonstrate that the project has been analyzed according to appropriate space needs standards and also consider the following criteria in determining whether to make a positive review and comment.</p>
          <p>(a) To grant a positive review and comment the commissioner shall determine that all of the following conditions are met:</p>
          <p>(1) the facilities are needed for pupils for whom no adequate facilities exist or will exist;</p>
          <p>(2) there is evidence to indicate that the facilities will have a useful public purpose for at least the term of the bonds;</p>
          <p>(3) no form of cooperation with another district would provide the necessary facilities;</p>
          <p>(4) the facilities are comparable in size and quality to facilities recently constructed in other districts that have similar enrollments;</p>
          <p>(5) the facilities are comparable in size and quality to facilities recently constructed in other districts that are financed without a capital loan;</p>
          <p>(6) the district is projected to have adequate funds in its general operating budget to support a quality education for its students for at least the next five years;</p>
          <p>(7) the current facility poses a threat to the life, health, and safety of pupils, and cannot reasonably be brought into compliance with fire, health, or life safety codes;</p>
          <p>(8) the district has made a good faith effort, as evidenced by its maintenance expenditures, to adequately maintain the existing facility during the previous ten years and to comply with fire, health, and life safety codes and state and federal requirements for accessibility for people with disabilities;</p>
          <p>(9) the district has made a good faith effort to encourage integration of social service programs within the new facility;</p>
          <p>(10) evaluations by boards of adjacent districts have been received; and</p>
          <p>(11) the proposal includes a comprehensive technology plan that assures information access for the students, parents, and community.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may grant a negative review and comment if:</p>
          <p>(1) the state demographer has examined the population of the communities to be served by the facility and determined that the communities have not grown during the previous five years;</p>
          <p>(2) the state demographer determines that the economic and population bases of the communities to be served by the facility are not likely to grow or to remain at a level sufficient, during the next ten years, to ensure use of the entire facility;</p>
          <p>(3) the need for facilities could be met within the district or adjacent districts at a comparable cost by leasing, repairing, remodeling, or sharing existing facilities or by using temporary facilities;</p>
          <p>(4) the district plans do not include cooperation and collaboration with health and human services agencies and other political subdivisions; or</p>
          <p>(5) if the application is for new construction, an existing facility that would meet the district's needs could be purchased at a comparable cost from any other source within the area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Multiple district proposals; review and comment.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the requirements of subdivision 3, the commissioner may use additional requirements to determine a positive review and comment on projects that are designed to serve more than one district. These requirements may include:</p>
          <p>(1) reducing or increasing the number of districts that plan to use the facility;</p>
          <p>(2) location of the facility; and</p>
          <p>(3) formation of a joint powers agreement among the participating districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Adjacent district comments.</span></h2>
          <p>The district must present the proposed project to the board of each adjacent district at a public meeting of that district. The board of an adjacent district must make a written evaluation of how the project will affect the future education and building needs of the adjacent district. The board must submit the evaluation to the applying district within 30 days of the meeting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">District application for capital grant and loan.</span></h2>
          <p>The school board of a district desiring a capital grant and loan shall adopt a resolution stating the amount proposed to be funded, the purpose for which the funding is requested, and an estimate of the dates when the facilities for which the funding is requested will be contracted for and completed. Applications for grants and loans must be accompanied by a copy of the adopted board resolution and copies of the adjacent district evaluations. The commissioner shall retain the evaluation as part of a permanent record of the district submitting the evaluation.</p>
          <p>Applications must be in the form and accompanied by the additional data required by the commissioner. Applications must be received by the commissioner by September 1 of an odd-numbered year. A district must resubmit an application each odd-numbered year. Capital grant and loan applications that do not receive voter approval or are not approved in law cancel July 1 of the year following application. When an application is received, the commissioner shall obtain from the commissioner of revenue the information in the Revenue Department's official records that is required to be used in computing the debt limit of the district under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.53#stat.475.53.4">475.53, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Commissioner review; district proposals.</span></h2>
          <p>By November 1 of each odd-numbered year, the commissioner must review all applications for capital grants and loans that have received a positive review and comment. When reviewing applications, the commissioner must consider whether the criteria in subdivision 3 have been met. The commissioner may not approve an application if all of the required deadlines have not been met. The commissioner may either approve or reject an application for a capital grant and loan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Commissioner recommendations.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall examine and consider applications for capital grants and loans that have been approved and promptly notify any district rejected of the decision.</p>
          <p>The commissioner shall report each capital grant and loan that has been approved by the commissioner and that has received voter approval to the education committees of the legislature by January 1 of each even-numbered year. The commissioner must not report a capital grant and loan that has not received voter approval. The commissioner shall also report on the money remaining in the capital loan account and, if necessary, request that another bond issue be authorized.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Grant and loan amount limits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A grant and loan must not be recommended for approval for a district exceeding an amount computed as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount requested by the district under subdivision 6;</p>
          <p>(2) plus the aggregate principal amount of general obligation bonds of the district outstanding on June 30 of the year following the year the application was received, not exceeding the limitation on net debt of the district in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.53#stat.475.53.4">475.53, subdivision 4</a>, or 637 percent of its adjusted net tax capacity as most recently determined, whichever is less;</p>
          <p>(3) less the maximum net debt permissible for the district on December 1 of the year the application is received, under the limitation in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.53#stat.475.53.4">475.53, subdivision 4</a>, or 637 percent of its adjusted net tax capacity as most recently determined, whichever is less;</p>
          <p>(4) less any amount by which the amount voted exceeds the total cost of the facilities for which the grant and loan is approved.</p>
          <p>(b) The grant and loan may be approved in an amount computed as provided in paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (3), subject to later reduction according to paragraph (a), clause (4).</p>
          <p>(c) The loan amount equals the lesser of the total grant and loan approved or:</p>
          <p>(1) the product of the maximum effort tax rate times 50 times the district's most recent adjusted net tax capacity at the time the capital grant and loan is approved under subdivision 10, minus</p>
          <p>(2) the district's capital loan balance outstanding at the time the capital grant and loan is approved under subdivision 10, minus</p>
          <p>(3) the district's principal and interest balance outstanding for eligible bonds issued for prior capital projects at the time the capital loan and grant is approved.</p>
          <p>(d) The grant amount equals the difference between the total grant and loan approved and the loan amount under paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Legislative action.</span></h2>
          <p>Each capital grant and loan must be approved in a law.</p>
          <p>If the aggregate amount of the capital grants and loans exceeds the amount that is or can be made available, the commissioner shall allot the available amount among any number of qualified applicant districts, according to the commissioner's judgment and discretion, based upon the districts' respective needs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">District referendum.</span></h2>
          <p>After receipt of the review and comment on the project and before January 1 of the even-numbered year, the question authorizing the borrowing of money for the facilities must be submitted by the school board to the voters of the district at a regular or special election. The question submitted must state the total amount to be borrowed from all sources. Approval of a majority of those voting on the question is sufficient to authorize the issuance of the obligations on public sale in accordance with chapter 475. The face of the ballot must include the following statement: "APPROVAL OF THIS QUESTION DOES NOT GUARANTEE THAT THE SCHOOL DISTRICT WILL RECEIVE A CAPITAL GRANT AND LOAN FROM THE STATE. THE GRANT AND LOAN MUST BE APPROVED BY THE STATE LEGISLATURE AND IS DEPENDENT ON AVAILABLE FUNDING." The district must mail to the commissioner a certificate by the clerk showing the vote at the election.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Contract.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each capital grant and loan must be evidenced by a contract between the district and the state acting through the commissioner. The contract must obligate the state to reimburse the district, from the maximum effort school loan fund, for eligible capital expenses for construction of the facility for which the grant and loan is approved, an amount computed as provided in subdivision 9. The commissioner must receive from the district a certified resolution of the board estimating the costs of construction and reciting that contracts for construction of the facilities for which the grant and loan is approved have been awarded, that bonds of the district have been issued and sold or that other district funds have been set aside in the amount necessary to pay all estimated costs of construction in excess of the amount of the grant and loan, and that all work, when completed, meets or exceeds standards established in the State Building Code. The contract must obligate the district to repay the loan out of the excesses of its maximum effort debt service levy over its required debt service levy. Beginning July 1, 2020, no interest assessments shall be made on capital loan balances.</p>
          <p>(b) The district must each year, as long as it is indebted to the state, levy for debt service (i) the amount of its maximum effort debt service levy or (ii) the amount of its required debt service levy, whichever is greater. The district shall remit payments to the commissioner according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.71">126C.71</a>. The actual debt service levy shall be adjusted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.09">477A.09</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall supervise the collection of outstanding accounts due the fund and may, by notice to the proper county auditor, require the maximum levy to be made as required in this subdivision. By September 30, the commissioner shall notify the county auditor of each county containing taxable property situated within the district of the amount of the maximum effort debt service levy of the district for that year. The county auditor or auditors shall extend upon the tax rolls an ad valorem tax upon all taxable property within the district in the aggregate amount so certified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Loan forgiveness.</span></h2>
          <p>If any capital loan is not paid within 50 years after it is granted from maximum effort debt service levies in excess of required debt service levies, the liability of the district on the loan is satisfied and discharged and interest on the loan ceases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Participation by county auditor; record of contract; payment of loan.</span></h2>
          <p>The district must file a copy of the capital loan contract with the county auditor of each county in which any part of the district is situated. The county auditor shall enter the capital loan, evidenced by the contract, in the auditor's bond register. The commissioner shall keep a record of each capital grant and loan contract showing the name and address of the district, the date of the contract, and the amount of the grant and loan initially approved. On receipt of the resolution required in subdivision 12 and documentation of expenditures under the contract, the commissioner shall issue payments on the capital grant and loan account for the amount that may be disbursed under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.69.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Bond sale limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district having an outstanding state loan must not issue and sell any bonds on the public market, except to refund state loans, unless it agrees to make the maximum effort debt service levy in each later year at the higher rate provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.63#stat.126C.63.8">126C.63, subdivision 8</a>, and unless it schedules the maturities of the bonds according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.54#stat.475.54.2">475.54, subdivision 2</a>. A district that refunds bonds at a lower interest rate may continue to make the maximum effort debt service levy in each later year at the current rate provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.63#stat.126C.63.8">126C.63, subdivision 8</a>, if the district can demonstrate to the commissioner's satisfaction that the district's repayments of the state loan will not be reduced below the previous year's level. The district must report each sale to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) For a capital loan issued prior to July 1, 2001, after the district's capital loan has been outstanding for 30 years, the district must not issue bonds on the public market except to refund the loan.</p>
          <p>(c) For a capital loan issued on or after July 1, 2001, after the district's capital loan has been outstanding for 20 years, the district must not issue bonds on the public market except to refund the loan.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.69.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.11.5.0">1990 c 562 art 11 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.5.6.0">1992 c 499 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.5.14.0">1993 c 224 art 5 s 14</a>-17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/2/#laws.1.27.0">1Sp1995 c 2 art 1 s 27</a>-31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.1.16.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 1 s 16</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.4.13.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 4 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.54.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 54</a>-62,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.4.16.0">1999 c 241 art 4 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.5.12.0">2000 c 489 art 5 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 2 s 21</a>-25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.1.55.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 1 s 55</a> subd 2; art 4 s 17,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.4.20.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 4 s 20</a>,21, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/292/#laws.1.12.0">2012 c 292 art 1 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.40.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.9.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.70">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.70 PREPAYMENTS.</h1>
        <p>A district may at any time pay the entire principal or part thereof and interest then due on a note or contract held by the state, out of any moneys not needed for school purposes. The district may issue and sell its refunding bonds in accordance with chapter 475, for such purpose, by actions of its school board and without the necessity of a vote by its electors, if such refunding bonds plus its net debt does not exceed the debt limit prescribed by chapter 475. Any such refunding bonds may bear interest at a rate or rates higher or lower than the rate payable on the loan or loans refunded thereby.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/27/#laws.0.9.0">Ex1959 c 27 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/752/#laws.0.7.0">1961 c 752 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.63.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 63</a>,164</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.71">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.71 PAYMENT AND APPLICATIONS OF PAYMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.71.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Payment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On November 20 of each year, each district having an outstanding capital loan or debt service loan shall compute the excess amount in the debt redemption fund. The commissioner shall prescribe the form and calculation to be used in computing the excess amount. A completed copy of this form shall be sent to the commissioner before December 1 of each year. The commissioner may recompute the excess amount and shall promptly notify the district of the recomputed amount.</p>
          <p> On December 15 of each year, the district shall remit to the commissioner, at a minimum, an amount equal to the greater of:</p>
          <p>(i) the excess amount in the debt redemption fund; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the amount by which the maximum effort debt service levy exceeds the required debt service levy for that calendar year.</p>
          <p>(b) If a payment required under paragraph (a) is not made within 30 days, the commissioner may reduce any subsequent payments due the district under this chapter and chapters 120B, 122A, 123A, 123B, 124D, 125A, and 127A by the amount due, after providing written notice to the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.71.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application of payments.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall apply payments received under subdivision 1 toward the principal of its contracts. While more than one contract is held, priority of payment must be given to the one of earliest date.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.71.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/27/#laws.0.10.0">Ex1959 c 27 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/432/#laws.0.62.0">1975 c 432 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.4.15.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.64.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 64</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/5/3/#laws.5.10.0">5Sp2020 c 3 art 5 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.126C.72">
        <h1 class="shn">126C.72 ISSUANCE AND SALE OF BONDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.72.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Certification.</span></h2>
          <p>On or before October 1 in each year, the commissioner shall certify to the commissioner of management and budget the amount anticipated to be needed for debt service loans and capital loans to be made under the Maximum Effort School Aid Law prior to October 1 in the following year. Each such certification of the commissioner shall also state an estimate of the dates and amounts the certified amount will be needed in the maximum effort school loan fund and an estimate as to the years and amounts in which payments on debt service loans and capital loans will be received.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.72.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Issuance and sale of bonds; commissioner of management and budget.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receipt of each such certification, subject to authorization as provided in subdivision 4, the commissioner of management and budget shall from time to time as needed issue and sell state of Minnesota school loan bonds in the aggregate principal amount stated in the commissioner's certificate, for the prompt and full payment of which, with the interest thereon, the full faith, credit, and taxing powers of the state are hereby irrevocably pledged. The commissioner of management and budget shall credit the net proceeds of the sale of the bonds to the purposes for which they are appropriated by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.66#stat.126C.66.1">126C.66, subdivision 1</a>. The bonds shall be issued and sold at such price, in such manner, in such number of series, at such times, and in such form and denominations, shall bear such dates of issue and of maturity, either without option of prior redemption or subject to prepayment upon such notice and at such times and prices, shall bear interest at such rate or rates and payable at such intervals, shall be payable at such bank or banks within or without the state, with such provisions for registration, conversion, and exchange, and for the issuance of notes in anticipation of the sale and delivery of definitive bonds, and in accordance with such further provisions as the commissioner of management and budget shall determine subject to the limitations stated in this subdivision (but not subject to chapter 14, including section 14.386). The maturity date must not be more than 20 years after the date of issue of any bond and the principal amounts. The due dates must conform as near as may be with the commissioner's estimates of dates and amounts of payments to be received on debt service and capital loans. The bonds and any interest coupons attached to them must be executed by the commissioner of management and budget under official seal. The signature of the commissioner and the seal may be printed, lithographed, stamped, engraved, or otherwise reproduced thereon. Each bond must be authenticated by the manual signature on its face of the commissioner or a person authorized to sign on behalf of a bank or trust company designated by the commissioner to act as registrar or other authenticating agent. The commissioner of management and budget is authorized and directed to ascertain and certify to purchasers of the bonds the performance and existence of all acts, conditions, and things necessary to make them valid and binding general obligations of the state of Minnesota in accordance with their terms.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.72.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">School loan bond account.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall maintain a separate school loan bond account in the state bond fund, showing all money transferred to that fund for the payment of school loan bonds and all income received from the investment of such money. On December 1, the commissioner of management and budget shall transfer to the bond account as much of the money then on hand in the loan repayment account in the maximum effort school loan fund as will be sufficient, with the balance then on hand in said bond account, to pay all principal and interest due and to become due within the next ensuing year and July 1 in the second ensuing year on school loan bonds issued and sold pursuant to this section. If money is not available for the transfer in the full amount required, and if any principal or interest on school loan bonds should become due at any time when there is not on hand a sufficient amount from any of the sources herein appropriated for the payment thereof, the moneys must be paid out of the general fund in the state treasury according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.641">16A.641</a>, and the amount necessary therefor is hereby appropriated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.126C.72.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Authority for issuance of bonds.</span></h2>
          <p>Bonds shall be issued pursuant to this section only when authorized by a law specifying the purpose thereof and the maximum amount of the proceeds authorized to be expended for that purpose. Any act authorizing the issuance of bonds in the manner provided in this section shall, together with this section, constitute complete authority for the issue, and the bonds shall not be subject to the restrictions or limitations contained in any other law. Bonds issued pursuant hereto may be sold at public or private sale and shall be deemed "authorized securities" within the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/50.14">50.14</a> and acts amendatory thereof or supplemental thereto.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.126C.72.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/27/#laws.0.12.0">Ex1959 c 27 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/601/#laws.0.4.0">1963 c 601 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/875/#laws.0.13.0">1965 c 875 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/399/#laws.0.49.0">1969 c 399 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/509/#laws.0.34.0">1980 c 509 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/607/#laws.14.29.0">1980 c 607 art 14 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.135.0">1983 c 301 s 135</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.4.22.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 4 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/45/#laws.0.4.0">1991 c 45 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.5.4.0">1994 c 647 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.56.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.5.17.0">1997 c 187 art 5 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.65.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 65</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.19.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
    </div>
